{\footnotesize\ttfamily struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__buffer}{trace\+\_\+buffer}}$\ast$ \+\_\+\+\_\+ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+alloc (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{size,  }\item[{unsigned}]{flags,  }\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structlock__class__key}{lock\+\_\+class\+\_\+key}} $\ast$}]{key }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\+\_\+\+\_\+ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+alloc -\/ allocate a new ring\+\_\+buffer @size\+: the size in bytes per cpu that is needed. @flags\+: attributes to set for the ring buffer. @key\+: ring buffer reader\+\_\+lock\+\_\+key.

Currently the only flag that is available is the R\+B\+\_\+\+F\+L\+\_\+\+O\+V\+E\+R\+W\+R\+I\+TE flag. This flag means that the buffer will overwrite old data when the buffer wraps. If this flag is not set, the buffer will drop data when the tail hits the head. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{ring\_buffer\_alloc\_read\_page()}{ring\_buffer\_alloc\_read\_page()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void$\ast$ ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+read\+\_\+page (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__buffer}{trace\+\_\+buffer}} $\ast$}]{buffer,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}}}]{cpu }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+read\+\_\+page -\/ allocate a page to read from buffer @buffer\+: the buffer to allocate for. @cpu\+: the cpu buffer to allocate.

This function is used in conjunction with ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+page. When reading a full page from the ring buffer, these functions can be used to speed up the process. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} calling function should allocate a few pages first with this function. Then when it needs to get pages from the ring buffer, it passes the result of this function into ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+page, which will swap the page that was allocated, with the read page of the buffer.

Returns\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} page allocated, or E\+R\+R\+\_\+\+P\+TR Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily void ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+discard\+\_\+commit (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__buffer}{trace\+\_\+buffer}} $\ast$}]{buffer,  }\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structring__buffer__event}{ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+event}} $\ast$}]{event }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+discard\+\_\+commit -\/ discard an event that has not been committed @buffer\+: the ring buffer @event\+: non committed event to discard

Sometimes an event that is in the ring buffer needs to be ignored. This function lets the user discard an event in the ring buffer and then that event will not be read later.

This function only works if it is called before the item has been committed. It will try to free the event from the ring buffer if another event has not been added behind it.

If another event has been added behind it, it will set the event up as discarded, and perform the commit.

If this function is called, do not call ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+unlock\+\_\+commit on the event. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{actypes_8h_ae9add973f3ccbe0212adbfea3b68be67}{u64}} ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+event\+\_\+time\+\_\+stamp (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__buffer}{trace\+\_\+buffer}} $\ast$}]{buffer,  }\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structring__buffer__event}{ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+event}} $\ast$}]{event }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+event\+\_\+time\+\_\+stamp -\/ return the event\textquotesingle{}s current time stamp @buffer\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} buffer that the event is on @event\+: the event to get the time stamp of

Note, this must be called after @event is reserved, and before it is committed to the ring buffer. And must be called from the same context where the event was reserved (normal, softirq, irq, etc).

Returns the time stamp associated with the current event. If the event has an extended time stamp, then that is used as the time stamp to return. In the highly unlikely case that the event was nested more than the max nesting, then the write\+\_\+stamp of the buffer is returned, otherwise current time is returned, but that really neither of the last two cases should ever happen. ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+nest\+\_\+start -\/ Allow to trace while nested @buffer\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} ring buffer to modify

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} ring buffer has a safety mechanism to prevent recursion. But there may be a case where a trace needs to be done while tracing something else. In this case, calling this function will allow this function to nest within a currently active \mbox{\hyperlink{trace_2ring__buffer_8c_a7a32782ece713469d8829346e2f6c95f}{ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+lock\+\_\+reserve()}}.

Call this function before calling another \mbox{\hyperlink{trace_2ring__buffer_8c_a7a32782ece713469d8829346e2f6c95f}{ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+lock\+\_\+reserve()}} and call \mbox{\hyperlink{trace_2ring__buffer_8c_a81bdb727dcd44698cc4b45e73db989e7}{ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+nest\+\_\+end()}} after the nested \mbox{\hyperlink{trace_2ring__buffer_8c_a20ec4cddc95ceaec074dc43331604880}{ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+unlock\+\_\+commit()}}. \doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{ring\_buffer\_read\_page()}{ring\_buffer\_read\_page()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+page (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__buffer}{trace\+\_\+buffer}} $\ast$}]{buffer,  }\item[{void $\ast$$\ast$}]{data\+\_\+page,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_aad0f8411dfc957b4069125f330192f4f}{size\+\_\+t}}}]{len,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}}}]{cpu,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}}}]{full }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+page -\/ extract a page from the ring buffer @buffer\+: buffer to extract from @data\+\_\+page\+: the page to use allocated from ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+read\+\_\+page @len\+: amount to extract @cpu\+: the cpu of the buffer to extract @full\+: should the extraction only happen when the page is full.

This function will pull out a page from the ring buffer and consume it. @data\+\_\+page must be the address of the variable that was returned from ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+read\+\_\+page. This is because the page might be used to swap with a page in the ring buffer.

for example\+: rpage = ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+read\+\_\+page(buffer, cpu); if (I\+S\+\_\+\+E\+R\+R(rpage)) return P\+T\+R\+\_\+\+E\+R\+R(rpage); ret = ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+page(buffer, \&rpage, len, cpu, 0); if (ret $>$= 0) process\+\_\+page(rpage, ret);

When @full is set, the function will not return true unless the writer is off the reader page.

Note\+: it is up to the calling functions to handle sleeps and wakeups. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} ring buffer can be used anywhere in the kernel and can not blindly call wake\+\_\+up. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} layer that uses the ring buffer must be responsible for that.

Returns\+: $>$=0 if data has been transferred, returns the offset of consumed data. $<$0 if no data has been transferred. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 205

ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+prepare -\/ Prepare for a non consuming read of the buffer @buffer\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} ring buffer to read from @cpu\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} cpu buffer to iterate over @flags\+: gfp flags to use for memory allocation

This performs the initial preparations necessary to iterate through the buffer. Memory is allocated, buffer recording is disabled, and the iterator pointer is returned to the caller.

Disabling buffer recording prevents the reading from being corrupted. This is not a consuming read, so a producer is not expected.

After a sequence of ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+prepare calls, the user is expected to make at least one call to ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+prepare\+\_\+sync. Afterwards, ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+start is invoked to get things going for real.

This overall must be paired with ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+read\+\_\+finish. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+wait -\/ wait for input to the ring buffer @buffer\+: buffer to wait on @cpu\+: the cpu buffer to wait on @full\+: wait until the percentage of pages are available, if @cpu != R\+I\+N\+G\+\_\+\+B\+U\+F\+F\+E\+R\+\_\+\+A\+L\+L\+\_\+\+C\+P\+US

If @cpu == R\+I\+N\+G\+\_\+\+B\+U\+F\+F\+E\+R\+\_\+\+A\+L\+L\+\_\+\+C\+P\+US then the task will wake up as soon as data is added to any of the @buffer\textquotesingle{}s cpu buffers. Otherwise it will wait for data to be added to a specific cpu buffer. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{ring\_buffer\_write()}{ring\_buffer\_write()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+write (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__buffer}{trace\+\_\+buffer}} $\ast$}]{buffer,  }\item[{unsigned long}]{length,  }\item[{void $\ast$}]{data }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+write -\/ write data to the buffer without reserving @buffer\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} ring buffer to write to. @length\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} length of the data being written (excluding the event header) @data\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} data to write to the buffer.

This is like ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+lock\+\_\+reserve and ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+unlock\+\_\+commit as one function. If you already have the data to write to the buffer, it may be easier to simply call this function.

Note, like ring\+\_\+buffer\+\_\+lock\+\_\+reserve, the length is the length of the data and not the length of the event which would hold the header. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
